| --- | --- |
| ![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg/60px-Ambox_current_red_Asia_Australia.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail) | This section needs to be **updated**. The reason given is: **Recent developments and Russian influence**. Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information._(December 2022)_ |

Main articles: [List of political parties in the Central African Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_political_parties_in_the_Central_African_Republic "List of political parties in the Central African Republic"), [Elections in the Central African Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elections_in_the_Central_African_Republic "Elections in the Central African Republic"), and [Central African Republic Council of Ministers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_African_Republic_Council_of_Ministers "Central African Republic Council of Ministers")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/48/Visit_of_Faustin-Archange_Touad%C3%A9ra%2C_President_of_the_Central_African_Republic%2C_to_the_European_Commission_%28P067074-430913%2C_cropped%29.jpg/120px-Visit_of_Faustin-Archange_Touad%C3%A9ra%2C_President_of_the_Central_African_Republic%2C_to_the_European_Commission_%28P067074-430913%2C_cropped%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Visit_of_Faustin-Archange_Touad%C3%A9ra,_President_of_the_Central_African_Republic,_to_the_European_Commission_(P067074-430913,_cropped).jpg)

[Faustin-Archange Touadéra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faustin-Archange_Touad%C3%A9ra "Faustin-Archange Touadéra")

[President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_the_Central_African_Republic "President of the Central African Republic")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/be/Felix_Moloua.png/250px-Felix_Moloua.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Felix_Moloua.png)

[Félix Moloua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F%C3%A9lix_Moloua "Félix Moloua")

[Prime Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_the_Central_African_Republic "Prime Minister of the Central African Republic")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6b/Faustin-Archange_Touad%C3%A9ra%2C_Joe_Biden%2C_Jill_Biden.jpg/250px-Faustin-Archange_Touad%C3%A9ra%2C_Joe_Biden%2C_Jill_Biden.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Faustin-Archange_Touad%C3%A9ra,_Joe_Biden,_Jill_Biden.jpg) Central African Republic President [Faustin-Archange Touadéra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faustin_Touadera "Faustin Touadera") with US President [Joe Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Biden "Joe Biden"), and [Jill Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jill_Biden "Jill Biden"), 14 December 2022

[Politics of the Central African Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_the_Central_African_Republic "Politics of the Central African Republic") formally take place in a framework of a [presidential](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_system "Presidential system") [republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic "Republic"). In this system, the president is the [head of state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_heads_of_state_of_the_Central_African_Republic "List of heads of state of the Central African Republic"), with a prime minister as head of government. Executive power is exercised by the government. Legislative power is vested in both the government and parliament.[&#8202;](#ref-back-CIA-3)[\[11\]](#refnote-CIA)

Changes in government have occurred in recent years by three methods: violence, negotiations, and elections. A new constitution was approved by voters in a referendum held on 5 December 2004. The government was rated 'Partly Free' from 1991 to 2001 and from 2004 to 2013.[&#8202;](#ref-back-111-0)[\[109\]](#refnote-111) [V-Dem Democracy Indices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V-Dem_Democracy_Indices "V-Dem Democracy Indices") described Central African Republic as [autocratizing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_backsliding "Democratic backsliding") in 2024.[&#8202;](#ref-back-j496-0)[\[110\]](#refnote-j496)

The president is elected by popular vote for a six-year term, and the prime minister is appointed by the president. The president also appoints and presides over the [Council of Ministers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_African_Republic_Council_of_Ministers "Central African Republic Council of Ministers"), which initiates laws and oversees government operations. However, as of 2018, the official government is not in control of large parts of the country, which are governed by rebel groups.[&#8202;](#ref-back-113-0)[\[111\]](#refnote-113) Acting president since April 2016 is Faustin-Archange Touadéra who followed the interim government under Catherine Samba-Panza and interim Prime Minister [André Nzapayeké](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andr%C3%A9_Nzapayek%C3%A9 "André Nzapayeké").[&#8202;](#ref-back-profile-1)[\[9\]](#refnote-profile)

The [National Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Assembly_of_the_Central_African_Republic "National Assembly of the Central African Republic") ( _Assemblée Nationale_) has 140 members, elected for a five-year term using the two-round (or [run-off](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-round_system "Two-round system")) system.[&#8202;](#ref-back-CIA-4)[\[11\]](#refnote-CIA) As in many other former French colonies, the Central African Republic's legal system is based on [French law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_law "French law").[&#8202;](#ref-back-114-0)[\[112\]](#refnote-114) The Supreme Court, or _Cour Suprême_, is made up of judges appointed by the president. There is also a Constitutional Court, and its judges are also appointed by the president.[&#8202;](#ref-back-CIA-5)[\[11\]](#refnote-CIA) [Freedom of speech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_speech "Freedom of speech") is addressed in the constitution, but there have been incidents of government intimidation of the media.[&#8202;](#ref-back-2009_Human_Rights_Report-0)[\[113\]](#refnote-2009_Human_Rights_Report) A report by the [International Research & Exchanges Board](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Research_&_Exchanges_Board "International Research & Exchanges Board")'s media sustainability index noted that "the country minimally met objectives, with segments of the legal system and government opposed to a free media system".[&#8202;](#ref-back-2009_Human_Rights_Report-1)[\[113\]](#refnote-2009_Human_Rights_Report)

### Administrative divisions

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Central_African_Republic&action=edit&section=16 "Edit section: Administrative divisions")\]

Main articles: [Prefectures of the Central African Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prefectures_of_the_Central_African_Republic "Prefectures of the Central African Republic") and [Sub-prefectures of the Central African Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sub-prefectures_of_the_Central_African_Republic "Sub-prefectures of the Central African Republic")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/Prefectures_of_the_Central_African_Republic.svg/330px-Prefectures_of_the_Central_African_Republic.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Prefectures_of_the_Central_African_Republic.svg) Prefectures of the Central African Republic

The Central African Republic is divided into 7 administrative regions, the regions are further divided into 20 administrative [prefectures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prefecture "Prefecture") ( _préfectures_) and 85 sub-prefectures ( _sous-préfectures_).[&#8202;](#ref-back-116-0)[\[114\]](#refnote-116)[&#8202;](#ref-back-117-0)[\[115\]](#refnote-117)

### Foreign relations

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Central_African_Republic&action=edit&section=17 "Edit section: Foreign relations")\]

Main article: [Foreign relations of the Central African Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_the_Central_African_Republic "Foreign relations of the Central African Republic")

See also: [Central African Armed Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_African_Armed_Forces "Central African Armed Forces")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/Vladimir_Putin_and_Faustin-Archange_Touadera_at_the_Third_Russia-Africa_Summit_%28October_2026%29.jpg/250px-Vladimir_Putin_and_Faustin-Archange_Touadera_at_the_Third_Russia-Africa_Summit_%28October_2026%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Vladimir_Putin_and_Faustin-Archange_Touadera_at_the_Third_Russia-Africa_Summit_(October_2026).jpg) President [Faustin-Archange Touadéra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faustin_Touadera "Faustin Touadera") with Russian President [Vladimir Putin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir_Putin "Vladimir Putin") at the [Russia–Africa Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Russia%E2%80%93Africa_Summit "2026 Russia–Africa Summit"), 28 October 2026

The Central African Republic is heavily dependent on foreign aid, and numerous [NGOs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-governmental_organization "Non-governmental organization") provide services that the government does not provide.[&#8202;](#ref-back-118-0)[\[116\]](#refnote-118) In 2019, over US$100million in foreign aid was spent in the country, mostly on humanitarian assistance.[&#8202;](#ref-back-119-0)[\[117\]](#refnote-119)

In 2006, due to ongoing violence, over 50,000 people in the country's northwest were at risk of starvation,[&#8202;](#ref-back-120-0)[\[118\]](#refnote-120) but this was averted due to assistance from the [United Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations "United Nations").[&#8202;](#ref-back-UNreport-0)[\[119\]](#refnote-UNreport) On 8 January 2008, the UN Secretary-General Ban Ki-Moon declared that the Central African Republic was eligible to receive assistance from the Peacebuilding Fund.[&#8202;](#ref-back-122-0)[\[120\]](#refnote-122) Three priority areas were identified: first, the reform of the security sector; second, the promotion of good governance and the rule of law; and third, the revitalization of communities affected by conflicts. On 12 June 2008, the Central African Republic requested assistance from the [UN Peacebuilding Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Peacebuilding_Commission "UN Peacebuilding Commission"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-123-0)[\[121\]](#refnote-123) which was set up in 2005 to help countries emerging from conflict avoid devolving back into war or chaos.[&#8202;](#ref-back-124-0)[\[122\]](#refnote-124)

In response to concerns of a potential genocide, a peacekeeping force—the International Support Mission to the Central African Republic (MISCA)—was authorized in December 2013. This African Union force of 6,000 personnel was accompanied by the French Operation Sangaris.[&#8202;](#ref-back-odi.org-0)[\[123\]](#refnote-odi.org)

In 2017, the Central African Republic signed the UN [Treaty on the Prohibition of Nuclear Weapons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_on_the_Prohibition_of_Nuclear_Weapons "Treaty on the Prohibition of Nuclear Weapons").[&#8202;](#ref-back-126-0)[\[124\]](#refnote-126)

In August 2025, Russia demanded that the Central African Republic pay its [Africa Corps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Africa_Corps_(Russia) "Africa Corps (Russia)") for armed protection rather than use the private mercenaries [Wagner Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wagner_Group "Wagner Group").[&#8202;](#ref-back-127-0)[\[125\]](#refnote-127)

### Human rights

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Central_African_Republic&action=edit&section=18 "Edit section: Human rights")\]

Main article: [Human rights in the Central African Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_in_the_Central_African_Republic "Human rights in the Central African Republic")

Further information: [LGBTQ rights in the Central African Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQ_rights_in_the_Central_African_Republic "LGBTQ rights in the Central African Republic") and [Polygamy in the Central African Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polygamy_in_the_Central_African_Republic "Polygamy in the Central African Republic")

The 2009 Human Rights Report by the [United States Department of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_State "United States Department of State") notes that human rights in the Central African Republic were poor and expressed concerns over numerous government abuses.[&#8202;](#ref-back-2009_Human_Rights_Report-2)[\[113\]](#refnote-2009_Human_Rights_Report) The [U.S. State Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_State_Department "United States State Department") alleged that major human rights abuses such as [extrajudicial executions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extrajudicial_punishment "Extrajudicial punishment") by security forces, torture, beatings, and rape of suspects and prisoners occurred with impunity. It also alleged harsh and life-threatening conditions in prison and detention centers, arbitrary arrest, prolonged pretrial detention and denial of a fair trial, restrictions on freedom of movement, official corruption, and restrictions on workers' rights.[&#8202;](#ref-back-2009_Human_Rights_Report-3)[\[113\]](#refnote-2009_Human_Rights_Report)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fb/Living_on_the_rainforest.jpg/250px-Living_on_the_rainforest.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Living_on_the_rainforest.jpg) [Aka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aka_people "Aka people") hunter-gatherers living in the [Dzanga-Sangha Special Reserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dzanga-Sangha_Special_Reserve "Dzanga-Sangha Special Reserve")

The State Department report also cites widespread mob violence, the prevalence of [female genital mutilation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Female_genital_mutilation "Female genital mutilation"), discrimination against women and [pygmies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pygmy_peoples "Pygmy peoples"), human trafficking, forced labor, and child labor.[&#8202;](#ref-back-128-0)[\[126\]](#refnote-128) Freedom of movement is limited in the northern part of the country "because of actions by state security forces, armed bandits, and other non-state armed entities", and due to fighting between government and anti-government forces, many people have been internally displaced.[&#8202;](#ref-back-state.gov-0)[\[127\]](#refnote-state.gov) Violence against children and women in relation to accusations of [witchcraft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Witchcraft "Witchcraft") has also been cited as a serious problem in the country.[&#8202;](#ref-back-unicef.org-0)[\[128\]](#refnote-unicef.org)[&#8202;](#ref-back-131-0)[\[129\]](#refnote-131)[&#8202;](#ref-back-132-0)[\[130\]](#refnote-132) Witchcraft is a criminal offense under the penal code.[&#8202;](#ref-back-unicef.org-1)[\[128\]](#refnote-unicef.org)

Approximately 68% of girls are married before they turn 18,[&#8202;](#ref-back-133-0)[\[131\]](#refnote-133) and the United Nations's [Human Development Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Development_Index "Human Development Index") ranked the country 188th out of 188 countries surveyed.[&#8202;](#ref-back-134-0)[\[132\]](#refnote-134) The [Bureau of International Labor Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bureau_of_International_Labor_Affairs "Bureau of International Labor Affairs") has also mentioned it in its last edition of the _[List of Goods Produced by Child Labor or Forced Labor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Goods_Produced_by_Child_Labor_or_Forced_Labor "List of Goods Produced by Child Labor or Forced Labor")_.

## Economy

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Central_African_Republic&action=edit&section=19 "Edit section: Economy")\]

Main article: [Economy of the Central African Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_the_Central_African_Republic "Economy of the Central African Republic")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a1/Central_African_Republic_GDP_Per_Capita_1960-2022.png/250px-Central_African_Republic_GDP_Per_Capita_1960-2022.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Central_African_Republic_GDP_Per_Capita_1960-2022.png) GDP per capita (2022 USD)[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/48/Bangui_Shopping_District.jpg/250px-Bangui_Shopping_District.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Bangui_Shopping_District.jpg) [Bangui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangui "Bangui") shopping district

The [per capita income](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Per_capita_income "Per capita income") of the Republic is often listed as being approximately $400 per year, [one of the lowest in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GNI_(PPP)_per_capita "List of countries by GNI (PPP) per capita"), but this figure is based mostly on reported sales of exports and largely ignores the unregistered sale of foods, locally produced [alcoholic beverages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alcoholic_beverage "Alcoholic beverage"), [diamonds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diamond "Diamond"), [ivory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivory "Ivory"), [bushmeat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bushmeat "Bushmeat"), and [traditional medicine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traditional_medicine "Traditional medicine").[&#8202;](#ref-back-:2-0)[\[133\]](#refnote-:2) The annual [growth rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_growth "Economic growth") of real GDP is slightly above 3%. The primary import partner is France (17.1%). Other imports come from the United States (12.3%), India (11.5%), and China (8.2%). Its largest export partner is France (31.2%), followed by [Burundi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burundi "Burundi") (16.2%), China (12.5%), [Cameroon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cameroon "Cameroon") (9.6%), and Austria (7.8%).[&#8202;](#ref-back-CIA-6)[\[11\]](#refnote-CIA) The currency is the [CFA franc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_African_CFA_franc "Central African CFA franc"), which is accepted across the former countries of [French West Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_West_Africa "French West Africa") and trades at a fixed rate to the euro.

Diamonds constitute the most important export, accounting for 40–55% of export revenues, but it is estimated that between 30% and 50% of those produced each year leave the country clandestinely.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:2-1)[\[133\]](#refnote-:2) Agriculture is dominated by the cultivation and sale of food crops such as [cassava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cassava "Cassava"), [peanuts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peanut "Peanut"), [maize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maize "Maize"), sorghum, millet, [sesame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sesame "Sesame"), and [plantain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/True_plantains "True plantains"). The importance of food crops over exported cash crops is indicated by the fact that the total production of cassava, the staple food of most Central Africans, ranges between 200,000 and 300,000 [tonnes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tonnes "Tonnes") per year, while the production of cotton, the principal exported cash crop, ranges from 25,000 to 45,000 tonnes per year. Food crops are not exported in large quantities, but still constitute the principal cash crops of the country because Central Africans derive far more income from the periodic sale of surplus food crops than from exported cash crops such as cotton or coffee.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:2-2)[\[133\]](#refnote-:2) Much of the country is self-sufficient in food crops; however, [livestock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Livestock "Livestock") development is hindered by the presence of the [tsetse fly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsetse_fly "Tsetse fly").[&#8202;](#ref-back-136-0)[\[134\]](#refnote-136)

The Central African Republic is a member of the Organization for the Harmonization of Business Law in Africa ( [OHADA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OHADA "OHADA")). In the 2009 [World Bank Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Bank_Group "World Bank Group")'s report _Doing Business_, it was ranked 183rd out of 183 as regards 'ease of doing business', a [composite index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Composite_(finance) "Composite (finance)") which takes into account regulations that enhance business activity and those that restrict it.[&#8202;](#ref-back-137-0)[\[135\]](#refnote-137)

## Infrastructure

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Central_African_Republic&action=edit&section=20 "Edit section: Infrastructure")\]

### Transportation

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Central_African_Republic&action=edit&section=21 "Edit section: Transportation")\]

Main article: [Transportation in the Central African Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transportation_in_the_Central_African_Republic "Transportation in the Central African Republic")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/29/Central_African_Republic_-_Trucks_in_Bangui.jpg/250px-Central_African_Republic_-_Trucks_in_Bangui.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Central_African_Republic_-_Trucks_in_Bangui.jpg) Trucks in [Bangui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangui "Bangui")

Two trans-African automobile routes pass through the Central African Republic: the [Tripoli-Cape Town Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tripoli-Cape_Town_Highway "Tripoli-Cape Town Highway") and the [Lagos-Mombasa Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagos-Mombasa_Highway "Lagos-Mombasa Highway"). Bangui is the transport hub. As of 1999, eight roads connected the city to other main towns in the country, Cameroon, Chad, and South Sudan; of these, only the toll roads are paved. During the rainy season from July to October, some roads are impassable.[&#8202;](#ref-back-e200-0)[\[136\]](#refnote-e200)[&#8202;](#ref-back-139-0)[\[137\]](#refnote-139)

River ferries sail from the river port at Bangui to [Brazzaville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazzaville "Brazzaville") and [Zongo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zongo,_Sud-Ubangi_(DR_Congo) "Zongo, Sud-Ubangi (DR Congo)"). The river can be navigated most of the year between Bangui and Brazzaville. From Brazzaville, goods are transported by rail to [Pointe-Noire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pointe-Noire "Pointe-Noire"), Congo's Atlantic port.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Finanace-0)[\[138\]](#refnote-Finanace) The river port handles the overwhelming majority of the country's international trade and has a cargo handling capacity of 350,000 tons; it has 350 metres (1,150ft) length of [wharfs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wharfs "Wharfs") and 24,000 square metres (260,000sqft) of warehousing space.[&#8202;](#ref-back-e200-1)[\[136\]](#refnote-e200)

[Bangui M'Poko International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangui_M'Poko_International_Airport "Bangui M'Poko International Airport") is the only international airport. Since at least 2002, there have been plans to connect Bangui by rail to the [Transcameroon Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transcameroon_Railway "Transcameroon Railway").[&#8202;](#ref-back-141-0)[\[139\]](#refnote-141)

### Energy

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Central_African_Republic&action=edit&section=22 "Edit section: Energy")\]

See also: [List of power stations in the Central African Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_power_stations_in_the_Central_African_Republic "List of power stations in the Central African Republic")

The Central African Republic primarily uses [hydroelectricity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydroelectricity "Hydroelectricity") as there are few other low-cost resources for generating electricity.[&#8202;](#ref-back-142-0)[\[140\]](#refnote-142) Access to electricity is very limited with 15.6% of the population having electrification, 34.6% in urban areas and 1.5% in rural areas.[&#8202;](#ref-back-WorldFactbook-0)[\[141\]](#refnote-WorldFactbook)

### Communications

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Central_African_Republic&action=edit&section=23 "Edit section: Communications")\]

Main article: [Communications in the Central African Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communications_in_the_Central_African_Republic "Communications in the Central African Republic")

The CAR has active television services, radio stations, internet service providers, and mobile phone carriers; [Socatel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socatel "Socatel") is the country's leading provider of internet and mobile phone access. The primary governmental regulating bodies of telecommunications are the Ministère des Postes and Télécommunications et des Nouvelles Technologies. In addition, the CAR receives international support on telecommunication-related operations from ITU Telecommunication Development Sector ( [ITU-D](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ITU-D "ITU-D")) of the [International Telecommunication Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Telecommunication_Union "International Telecommunication Union") to improve infrastructure.[&#8202;](#ref-back-144-0)[\[142\]](#refnote-144)

## Demographics

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Central_African_Republic&action=edit&section=24 "Edit section: Demographics")\]

Main article: [Demographics of the Central African Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_the_Central_African_Republic "Demographics of the Central African Republic")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/84/Group_of_Peul_women_in_Paoua.jpg/250px-Group_of_Peul_women_in_Paoua.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Group_of_Peul_women_in_Paoua.jpg) Fula women in [Paoua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paoua "Paoua")

The population has almost quadrupled since independence. In 1960, the population was 1,232,000; as of a 2021 UN estimate, it is approximately 5,457,154.[&#8202;](#ref-back-UN_WPP-0)[\[143\]](#refnote-UN_WPP)[&#8202;](#ref-back-UN_WPP_2022-0)[\[144\]](#refnote-UN_WPP_2022) The nation comprises over 80 ethnic groups, each having its own language. The largest ethnic groups are the [Baggara Arabs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baggara "Baggara"), [Baka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baka_people_(Cameroon_and_Gabon) "Baka people (Cameroon and Gabon)"), [Banda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banda_people "Banda people"), [Bayaka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aka_people "Aka people"), [Fula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fula_people "Fula people"), [Gbaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gbaya_people "Gbaya people"), [Kara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kara_people "Kara people"), [Kresh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kresh_people "Kresh people"), [Mbaka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mbaka_people "Mbaka people"), [Mandja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandja_people "Mandja people"), [Ngbandi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ngbandi_people "Ngbandi people"), [Sara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sara_people "Sara people"), [Vidiri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vidiri_people "Vidiri people"), [Wodaabe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wodaabe "Wodaabe"), [Yakoma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yakoma_people "Yakoma people"), [Yulu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yulu_people "Yulu people"), and [Zande](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zande_people "Zande people"), with [others](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_the_Central_African_Republic#Ethnic_groups "Demographics of the Central African Republic") including Europeans of mostly [French descent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_people "French people").[&#8202;](#ref-back-CIA-7)[\[11\]](#refnote-CIA) The most common ethnic groups are Gbaya (Baya) (28.8%) and Banda (22.9%), comprising together slightly over half of the country's population in 2003.[&#8202;](#ref-back-147-0)[\[145\]](#refnote-147)

### Languages

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Central_African_Republic&action=edit&section=25 "Edit section: Languages")\]

Main article: [Languages of the Central African Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_of_the_Central_African_Republic "Languages of the Central African Republic")

Official languages are [French](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_language "French language") and [Sango](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sango_language "Sango language"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-149-0)[\[147\]](#refnote-149) a [creole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Creole_language "Creole language") developed as an inter-ethnic [lingua franca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lingua_franca "Lingua franca") based on the local [Ngbandi language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ngbandi_language "Ngbandi language").

### Religion

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Central_African_Republic&action=edit&section=26 "Edit section: Religion")\]

Main article: [Religion in the Central African Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_the_Central_African_Republic "Religion in the Central African Republic")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/91/Une_foule_devant_la_Cath%C3%A9drale_de_Bangui%2C_le_7_mai_2018.jpg/250px-Une_foule_devant_la_Cath%C3%A9drale_de_Bangui%2C_le_7_mai_2018.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Une_foule_devant_la_Cath%C3%A9drale_de_Bangui,_le_7_mai_2018.jpg) Worshippers at the [Bangui Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cath%C3%A9drale_Notre-Dame_(Bangui) "Cathédrale Notre-Dame (Bangui)"). [Christianity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity "Christianity") is the main religion in the Central African Republic.

According to the 2003 national census, 80.3% of the population was [Christian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity "Christianity") (51.4% [Protestant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestantism "Protestantism") and 28.9% [Roman Catholic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholicism "Roman Catholicism")), 10% was [Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam "Islam"), and 4.5 percent was other religious groups, with 5.5 percent having no religious beliefs.[&#8202;](#ref-back-150-0)[\[148\]](#refnote-150) In 2010 the Pew Research Center estimated that Christians constituted 89.8% of the population (60.7% Protestant and 28.5% Catholic) while Muslims made up 8.9%.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Pew_Christians-0)[\[149\]](#refnote-Pew_Christians)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Pew_Muslims-0)[\[150\]](#refnote-Pew_Muslims) The Catholic Church claims over 1.5million adherents, approximately one-third of the population.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Catholic_Stats-0)[\[151\]](#refnote-Catholic_Stats) Indigenous belief ( [animism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Animism "Animism")) is also practiced, and many indigenous beliefs are [incorporated into](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religious_syncretism "Religious syncretism") Christian and Islamic practice.[&#8202;](#ref-back-154-0)[\[152\]](#refnote-154) A UN director described religious tensions between Muslims and Christians as being high.[&#8202;](#ref-back-155-0)[\[153\]](#refnote-155)

Many missionary groups are operating in the country, including [Lutherans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lutheran "Lutheran"), [Baptists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baptists "Baptists"), [Catholics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic "Catholic"), [Grace Brethren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fellowship_of_Grace_Brethren_Churches "Fellowship of Grace Brethren Churches"), and [Jehovah's Witnesses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jehovah's_Witnesses "Jehovah's Witnesses"). While these missionaries are predominantly from the United States, France, Italy, and Spain, many are also from [Nigeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigeria "Nigeria"), the [Democratic Republic of the Congo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Republic_of_the_Congo "Democratic Republic of the Congo"), and other African countries. Large numbers of missionaries left the country when fighting broke out between rebel and government forces in 2002, but many of them have returned to continue their work.[&#8202;](#ref-back-156-0)[\[154\]](#refnote-156) According to Overseas Development Institute research, during the crisis ongoing since 2012, religious leaders have mediated between communities and armed groups; they also provided refuge for people seeking shelter.[&#8202;](#ref-back-odi.org-1)[\[123\]](#refnote-odi.org)

### Education

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Central_African_Republic&action=edit&section=27 "Edit section: Education")\]

Main article: [Education in the Central African Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_the_Central_African_Republic "Education in the Central African Republic")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/00/CAR_classroom.jpg/250px-CAR_classroom.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:CAR_classroom.jpg) Classroom in [Sam Ouandja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sam_Ouandja "Sam Ouandja")

Public education is free and compulsory from ages 6 to 14,[&#8202;](#ref-back-ilab-0)[\[155\]](#refnote-ilab) but approximately half of the adult population is illiterate.[&#8202;](#ref-back-158-0)[\[156\]](#refnote-158) The two institutions of higher education are the [University of Bangui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Bangui "University of Bangui"), a public university in Bangui that includes a [medical school](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medical_school "Medical school"); and [Euclid University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euclid_University "Euclid University"), an international university.[&#8202;](#ref-back-159-0)[\[157\]](#refnote-159)[&#8202;](#ref-back-160-0)[\[158\]](#refnote-160) Only 66% of CAR kids attend school.[&#8202;](#ref-back-161-0)[\[159\]](#refnote-161)

### Health

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Central_African_Republic&action=edit&section=28 "Edit section: Health")\]

Main article: [Health in the Central African Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_in_the_Central_African_Republic "Health in the Central African Republic")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9f/Caf_babies.jpg/250px-Caf_babies.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Caf_babies.jpg) Mothers with babies aged between 0 and 5 years are lining up in a Health Post at Begoua, a district of [Bangui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangui "Bangui"), waiting for the two drops of the oral [polio vaccine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polio_vaccine "Polio vaccine").

The largest hospitals are in the Bangui district. As a member of the [World Health Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Health_Organization "World Health Organization"), the Central African Republic receives vaccination assistance, such as a 2014 intervention for the prevention of a measles epidemic.[&#8202;](#ref-back-162-0)[\[160\]](#refnote-162) In 2007, female [life expectancy at birth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Life_expectancy_at_birth "Life expectancy at birth") was 48.2 years, and male life expectancy at birth was 45.1 years.[&#8202;](#ref-back-hdrstats.undp.org-0)[\[161\]](#refnote-hdrstats.undp.org)As of 2010[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Central_African_Republic&action=edit), the country had the fourth highest [maternal mortality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maternal_mortality "Maternal mortality") rate in the world.[&#8202;](#ref-back-164-0)[\[162\]](#refnote-164) The [total fertility rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Total_fertility_rate "Total fertility rate") in 2014 was estimated at 4.46 children born/woman.[&#8202;](#ref-back-CIA-8)[\[11\]](#refnote-CIA) Approximately 25% of women had undergone [female genital mutilation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Female_genital_mutilation "Female genital mutilation").[&#8202;](#ref-back-165-0)[\[163\]](#refnote-165) Many births in the country are guided by traditional [birth attendants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Birth_attendant "Birth attendant"), who often have little or no formal training.[&#8202;](#ref-back-166-0)[\[164\]](#refnote-166)

[Malaria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malaria "Malaria") is endemic and one of the leading causes of death.[&#8202;](#ref-back-167-0)[\[165\]](#refnote-167) According to 2009 estimates, the HIV/AIDS prevalence rate is about 4.7% of the adult population (ages 15–49);[&#8202;](#ref-back-unaids-0)[\[166\]](#refnote-unaids)[&#8202;](#ref-back-169-0)[\[167\]](#refnote-169) 3% has [antiretroviral therapy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Management_of_HIV/AIDS "Management of HIV/AIDS") available, compared to 17% coverage in the neighboring countries of Chad and the Republic of the Congo.[&#8202;](#ref-back-170-0)[\[168\]](#refnote-170)\[ _[needs update](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Dates_and_numbers#Chronological_items "Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Dates and numbers")_\] Government expenditure on health was US$20 (PPP) per person in 2006[&#8202;](#ref-back-hdrstats.undp.org-1)[\[161\]](#refnote-hdrstats.undp.org) and 10.9% of total government expenditure in 2006.[&#8202;](#ref-back-hdrstats.undp.org-2)[\[161\]](#refnote-hdrstats.undp.org) There was only around 1 physician for every 20,000 people in 2009.[&#8202;](#ref-back-171-0)[\[169\]](#refnote-171)

With a score of 31.5 in the 2024 Global Hunger Index,[&#8202;](#ref-back-172-0)[\[170\]](#refnote-172) the Central African Republic ranks 119th out of the 127 countries with sufficient data to calculate 2024 GHI scores.

## Culture

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Central_African_Republic&action=edit&section=29 "Edit section: Culture")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/BaAka_woman_dancing.jpg/250px-BaAka_woman_dancing.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:BaAka_woman_dancing.jpg) A [Baka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baka_people_(Cameroon_and_Gabon) "Baka people (Cameroon and Gabon)") woman dancing

See also: [List of African writers by country §Central African Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_African_writers_by_country#Central_African_Republic "List of African writers by country"), and [Music of the Central African Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_the_Central_African_Republic "Music of the Central African Republic")

### Music

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Central_African_Republic&action=edit&section=30 "Edit section: Music")\]

According to UNESCO, “The Aka Pygmies living in the south-west region of the Central African Republic have developed a distinctive vocal musical tradition, which involves a complex type of contrapuntal polyphony based on four voices, mastered by all members of the Aka community.” [&#8202;](#ref-back-173-0)[\[171\]](#refnote-173)

### Sports

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Central_African_Republic&action=edit&section=31 "Edit section: Sports")\]

See also: [Central African Republic at the Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_African_Republic_at_the_Olympics "Central African Republic at the Olympics")

Football is the country's most popular sport. The [national football team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_African_Republic_national_football_team "Central African Republic national football team") is governed by the [Central African Football Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_African_Football_Federation "Central African Football Federation") and stages matches at the [Barthélemy Boganda Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barth%C3%A9lemy_Boganda_Stadium "Barthélemy Boganda Stadium").[&#8202;](#ref-back-174-0)[\[172\]](#refnote-174) Basketball is also popular[&#8202;](#ref-back-175-0)[\[173\]](#refnote-175)[&#8202;](#ref-back-176-0)[\[174\]](#refnote-176) and its [national team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_African_Republic_national_basketball_team "Central African Republic national basketball team") won the [African Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AfroBasket "AfroBasket") twice and was the first Sub-Saharan African team to qualify for the [Basketball World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basketball_World_Cup "Basketball World Cup"), in [1974](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1974_FIBA_World_Championship "1974 FIBA World Championship").
